# docs: AI-assisted rewrite of frontend documentation

[Upstream PR](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1815) · state: closed · merged: False

## Original PR description

## Before submitting

- [x] I agree to license this contribution under the terms of [LICENSE.txt](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/develop/LICENSE.txt).
- [x] I ran `pre-commit run` and committed any formatting changes.
- [x] I reviewed the Hard Rules in the `AGENTS.md` for each directory this PR touches and the changes comply.
- [x] I added GitHub labels: `cat-doc`, `area:packaging`, and `orig-nv-eng`.

## Affected area

Documentation or samples

## Summary

This is an AI-assisted rewrite of the cuDNN Frontend documentation. It:

- reorganizes the guide around tutorials, task-oriented how-to guides, concepts, and exact API reference material;
- expands graph-construction coverage from declaration through heuristic selection, execution-plan creation, execution, and graph reuse;
- provides paired Python and C++ examples wherever both interfaces support the workflow, including backward examples when applicable;
- consolidates and clarifies attention layouts, masks, precision modes, ragged tensors, graph operations, and supported fusion patterns;
- publishes 247 canonical sample sources and generated snippets instead of duplicating example code;
- inventories 277 individual API pages and 424 family-owned API candidates with source evidence and API-local availability contracts; and
- adds deterministic documentation coverage, generation, and sample-validation checks.

The rewrite used AI assistance for inventory work, restructuring, drafting, cross-language example construction, and review. Technical claims and examples were checked against repository sources, tests, and samples.

Fern-related changes only align this rewrite with the documentation generation and publication mechanism already present in the latest `develop` branch. Fern is supporting build infrastructure, not the primary purpose of this PR.

## Why

The existing documentation had coverage gaps, duplicated or incomplete examples, and inconsistent treatment of Python and C++. This rewrite makes graph workflows easier to learn, makes constraints discoverable at the API level, and gives maintainers deterministic checks for documentation drift.

## Related issues

None.

## API and compatibility impact

No runtime API or binary compatibility changes. The documentation snapshot and compatibility ledger target source commit `0a07d4ee28f8ae2f2106bb766b1878cc732ee80c`.

## Testing

- `PYENV_VERSION=pt python -m pytest tools/doc/tests -q` — 47 passed.
- `PYENV_VERSION=pt python tools/doc/build_doc_manifests.py --check` — 3 manifests current.
- `PYENV_VERSION=pt python tools/doc/build_doc_indexes.py --check` — 300 generated artifacts current.
- `PYENV_VERSION=pt python tools/doc/check_doc_coverage.py --mode release` — 277 APIs, 424 family-owned candidates, and 340 historical-topic dispositions covered.
- `PYENV_VERSION=pt python tools/doc/run_doc_samples.py --list` — 247 canonical samples cataloged.
- Fern validation — 0 errors; the unauthenticated redirect check emitted one expected warning.

- YangXu1990uiuc · APPROVED · 2026-10-23T21:13:52Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1815#pullrequestreview-5634510577)

**Codex bot review · model: `gpt-6-astra`**

Reviewed `f4f3ce6e0c50` against base `84eadd2074fa`.

The canonical-sample and generated-reference structure is useful. I found four localized issues: an unsafe cache example, generation inputs missing from clean checkouts, stale installation/verification metadata, and a rounded BF16 reference that fails in the causal-convolution sample. No P0 or high-risk P1 found in this documentation-only change. Please address these and restore the existing Docs/style checks before merging. CodeQL's non-callable warning for cudnn.ops.causal_conv1d was not reproduced: that entrypoint executes successfully; the sample fails later in its gradient comparison.

**[P2] Include or validate B's dtype and device before reusing the example graph** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/f4f3ce6e0c502e7ac08154206524e937857d8259/docs/samples/python/pytorch_cached_graph.py#L17))

The cache key records only A's dtype/device. With A=BF16 ones of shape (2,4,8) and B=BF16 twos of shape (1,8,6), the first call correctly returns 16. Replacing only B with B.float() produces the same key; the cached call silently returns alternating 0 and 16 instead of rejecting the incompatible input. A fresh build rejects that combination. This contradicts the integration guide's instruction to key every input's metadata. Include B's dtype/device in the key, or explicitly enforce homogeneous dtype/device before every lookup, and add a warm-cache mismatch regression.

Evidence: Independent SM100 execution of the actual published cached_matmul helper: BF16 control [16,16,16,16,16]; same-key FP32 B [0,16,0,16,0]; fresh graph rejects. Only the documentation helper is added by this PR; no runtime-library regression is attributed here. Attribution: New documentation sample; independently reproduced.

**[P2] Make manifest generation reproducible from a clean checkout** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/f4f3ce6e0c502e7ac08154206524e937857d8259/tools/doc/build_doc_manifests.py#L1571))

build_old_docs_ledger reads an old_docs/ tree that is not tracked in this PR. In a clean checkout it generates zero topics instead of the committed 340, so build_doc_manifests.py --check fails even with full Git history. The new Docs workflow hits another prerequisite first: checkout@v4's default shallow fetch lacks target_commit 0a07d4e, and source_target_commit raises from git cat-file. The current fern-check run confirms that failure. Track a reproducible historical inventory or reconstruct it from an explicit fetched revision, and fetch the pinned commit (or remove the unnecessary local-object requirement). Test --check from a fresh clone rather than an author workspace containing old_docs/.

Evidence: Local exact-head manifest check exits 1 with DRIFT: docs/old-docs-disposition.json; tracked old_docs files=0, generated topics=0, committed topics=340. Current Docs check https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/actions/runs/40020608709/job/120643887275 fails git cat-file for the pinned commit with exit 128. Attribution: New generator and workflow; independently reproduced plus current CI evidence.

**[P2] Reconcile the installation and verification ledger with the pinned source** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/f4f3ce6e0c502e7ac08154206524e937857d8259/docs/getting-started/installation.md#L7))

The new guide says Python 3.9, CMake 3.23, pybind11, and optional pinned CUTLASS DSL dependencies. At the stated source target 0a07d4e, pyproject.toml already requires Python >=3.10 and CUTLASS DSL >=4.6.2, the root CMake minimum is 3.26, and the binding build uses nanobind. docs/compatibility.json additionally says the extra pins DSL 4.5.0, while verification.md labels this checkout FE 1.27.0 although that pinned commit declares 1.32.0. Update the source-owned requirements and regenerate the repeated availability blocks; keep any historical verification environment explicitly separate from the current source/support contract.

Evidence: Read current and commit-pinned pyproject.toml, CMakeLists.txt and python/cudnn/__init__.py; the native environment probe resolves this PR's Python source and reports FE 1.32.0. The stale DSL statement is propagated by compatibility.json into generated reference pages. Attribution: New documentation claims disagree with their own pinned source.

**[P2] Use a sufficiently precise oracle for the causal-convolution gradient example** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/f4f3ce6e0c502e7ac08154206524e937857d8259/docs/samples/python/causal_conv1d.py#L55))

This executable sample fails the bias-gradient assertion on SM100 with cuDNN 9.27: one of 128 values differs by 0.0620117 against atol=0.05. Its reference computes conv1d and SiLU separately in BF16, introducing an intermediate rounding that the fused implementation need not share. In a separate probe on the same seeded inputs, computing the reference and gradients in FP32 passes the existing rtol/atol=0.05 checks for dx, dw and db. Please use the higher-precision oracle with explicit dtype handling and run through the later torch.compile section on a build that exposes the bindings; syntax fallback cannot qualify these numerical assertions. This is a sample/reference issue, not evidence of a newly introduced kernel bug.

Evidence: Native exact-head sample runner: causal_conv1d fails at gradient comparison, while five other selected Python examples pass. Independent FP32 reference probe passes all three gradient comparisons at the existing tolerances. The failing sample never reaches its subsequent torch.compile call. Attribution: New sample; independently reproduced on a platform with the bindings available.

Validation: 47 documentation-tool tests passed. All 300 generated indexes are current; release coverage check passes for 277 API pages, 424 candidates and 340 committed historical-topic rows. The manifest-generation check itself fails as described above; sample listing catalogs 247 entries but does not execute them. Native SM100, PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130, CUDA 13.0, CUTLASS DSL 4.8.0, cuDNN 9.27: five selected Python examples execute successfully (matmul, engine inspection/execution, RMSNorm backward, causal GQA forward/backward, environment probe); causal convolution fails its BF16 gradient oracle. The RMSNorm backward example checks execution/shapes, not a numerical reference. Compiled and executed the two C++ matmul and causal-GQA forward/backward examples against the current headers; both pass. Exact PR Python source was verified, using an existing compatible compiled extension; this PR changes no runtime bindings. Independent warm-cache dtype-transition and FP32 causal-gradient probes establish the two sample findings. The normal BF16 cache control computes the expected result. Inspected existing Docs, CodeQL and style checks. The current style job also reports formatting and missing SPDX headers in new samples; please run the changed-file hooks and commit their fixes. No duplicate GPU CI run requested for this documentation change. Existing labels/project retained and milestone set to Frontend 1.32.0.

Limitations: This was a structural/source-contract review plus focused sample execution, not native execution of all 247 samples or independent verification of every generated compatibility claim. No Windows qualification or independent Fern preview/publication. No application-performance or new kernel-determinism claim; runtime kernel implementations are unchanged.

Approved: no P0 or high-risk P1 found. Remaining findings stay with the owner; merge timing stays with the owner.

**Codex bot review · model: `gpt-6-astra`**

Reviewed `665c9a496aa5` against base `35150c04f317`.

Thank you for your contribution! Rechecked the update: the four earlier findings are addressed, and the custom operator's fake broadcast shape now works in compiled execution. Documentation generation and current Docs/style checks pass. One localized P2 remains in the newly exercised broadcast backward example; no P0 or high-risk P1 found. My existing approval stands.

**[P2] Keep broadcast-gradient partials in FP32 until after the batch reduction** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/665c9a496aa5b0ffcbc478eff1fcaf71f4518717/docs/samples/python/pytorch_custom_operator.py#L82))

The updated executable sample still fails at line 108 on SM100. With its own seed 11 and A[1,32,64], B[2,64,48], grad_a[0,23,24] is 0.0625 versus torch.matmul's 0.099609375 (independent FP32 oracle: 0.09945142), exceeding the existing rtol/atol=0.03. The per-batch matmul is materialized in BF16 before sum_to_size, so cancellation happens after rounding the partials. Reversing the broadcast direction also exposes this in grad_b. Please perform the gradient matmul and sum_to_size in FP32, then convert the final gradient to the corresponding input dtype, or use an equivalent accumulation strategy. An independent probe of that formula passes both directions at the unchanged tolerances. This is a sample/backward precision issue, not a new cuDNN kernel defect; the fake-shape fix and compiled forward both pass.

Evidence: Native exact-head sample fails 1/2048 gradient-A elements; reversed broadcast probe fails 2/3072 gradient-B elements. FP32 accumulation through the batch reduction reduces the maximum difference from Torch's BF16 gradients to 0 and 3.814697e-6 respectively. Existing numerical tolerances were not widened. Attribution: Newly exercised broadcast case and backward reduction in this follow-up; independently reproduced.

Validation: 47 documentation-tool tests passed; both manifest checks, all 300 generated artifacts, and release coverage pass. The 247-entry sample catalog is intact. Exact-head native validation on SM100 with Torch 2.14.1+cu130, CUDA 13.0, CUTLASS DSL 4.8.0 and cuDNN 9.27: cached matmul, causal convolution (eager/backward/compile), and serialization examples pass. The custom-op example has the localized gradient failure above; its compiled forward passes in both broadcast directions. Both updated graph-cache helpers reject a warm-cache BF16-to-FP32 change in B explicitly and continue to execute correctly when B returns to BF16. Confirmed the frozen historical ledger removes the missing old_docs dependency, and installation metadata now matches the stated source contract. Reviewed changes since the prior head, including the substantive sample/tooling edits; most other source changes are formatting/license headers. Runtime-library implementations are unchanged. Current Docs, pre-commit and CodeQL checks pass; no redundant GPU CI run requested.

Limitations: Focused follow-up validation, not native execution of all 247 samples or qualification of every documented platform. Existing compatible compiled bindings were used with verified PR Python source. No new application-performance or determinism claim. The gradient discrepancy above concerns intermediate rounding, not observed run-to-run nondeterminism.

Comment only: this PR already has an active approval; preserving existing approvals.

- Anerudhan · comment · 2026-10-23T22:56:41Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1815#issuecomment-6376376811)

Additional native sample validation for `f4f3ce6e0c50`, alongside [the completed review](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1815#pullrequestreview-5634510577): 117 Python samples executed successfully, one used its declared static fallback, and one failed at the already-reported causal-convolution reference comparison. C++ matmul and graph-cache samples also compiled and ran. These checks used exact-head source on SM100, Torch 2.14/cu130, CuTeDSL 4.7.1 and cuDNN 9.26.

**P2: match the custom operator's fake shape to eager batch broadcasting.** In [pytorch_custom_operator.py:69](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/f4f3ce6e0c502e7ac08154206524e937857d8259/docs/samples/python/pytorch_custom_operator.py#L69), `A[1,32,64]` and `B[2,64,48]` produce `[2,32,48]` eagerly, but the fake implementation declares `[1,32,48]`. The native `torch.compile` probe fails with `expected size 2==1`; the equal-batch sample passes. Infer the broadcast batch extent, or consistently reject unequal batches in both implementations, and include this case in the compiled checks. This is separate from the graph-cache dtype/device finding.

Codex bot review — model `gpt-6-astra`.
